<result>PR #476 is open and not merged: https://github.com/mikeholownych/nexus-code/pull/476

**Real mismatch found and fixed.** The REPL and sidecar surfaces each assembled their own facts, and four of the six fields did not agree:
- **Sidecar:** `session.state` had no read-only flag, context tokens used, context limit or endpoint kind.
- **REPL:** `/status` had no read-only flag, context tokens used or endpoint kind. It also showed the bare model where `session.state` showed `provider · model`.
- **REPL `/status` handler:** it is `printStatus` in `cli/src/repl.ts`, separate from the sidecar's slash `status` handler.

**Fix.**
- New `cli/src/surface_facts.ts` holds one `surfaceFacts()` that both surfaces now read.
- `session.state` gains optional `read_only`, `context_tokens`, `context_limit` and `endpoint_kind`.
- Verbose REPL `/status` gains `READ-ONLY` and `CONTEXT` lines and an endpoint-kind suffix.
- I did not rebuild or run the Rust TUI. Its deserializer ignores unknown fields, so the added fields are safe, and its status line renders the `session.state` event.

**Test.** `cli/test/surface_agreement.test.ts` drives one session through both surfaces and compares the six fields one by one:
- **Setup:** a read-only session with one turn through a fake SSE gateway.
- **Red first:** it failed with `REPL /status reports read_only`.
- **Green after the fix:** passes.
- **Mutation check:** inverting `read_only` in the sidecar turned it red with `read_only: REPL /status says yes, sidecar session.state says no`. I reverted the mutation.

**Test run.** `CI=true node scripts/run_tests.mjs` over `surface_agreement`, `sidecar`, `protocol`, `sidecar_surface`, `app`, `repl_readonly_commands` and `sidecar_projection` gave 102 tests, 102 pass, 0 fail. `tsc --noEmit` is clean.

**Existing test edited.** In `cli/test/sidecar.test.ts`, "an exception escaping runTurn" counts `getAgent()` calls to throw inside the turn. `emitSessionState` now calls `getAgent()` once instead of twice, so I changed the index from `calls === 3` to `calls === 2`. It passed at base before my change.

**FINDINGS.** Row I-3 in `docs/v1.1.4/FINDINGS.md` now reads FIXED with the test name. No other row was touched, and no status table mirrors it.

**Setup.**
- **`core/target`:** the worktree has none. I did not create the symlink or build it, because no test I ran needs the native core.
- **`cli/node_modules`:** the main checkout has none either. I ran `npm ci` inside the worktree's `cli/`, which is gitignored.</result>

<result>**ACCEPT** at c6e9275a. I posted the verdict as a PR #448 comment (https://github.com/mikeholownych/nexus-code/pull/448#issuecomment-6775154468) and removed the rev-448 worktree.

1. **Logic:** The new condition `state.total &gt; 0 &amp;&amp; noSquash` matches what `mergeWorktree` actually does.
   - With a dirty tree, `options.message` goes on the dirty commit.
   - The squash commit reuses it only when `!noSquash &amp;&amp; count &gt; 1`.
   - The single-commit amend runs only when `!dirty`.
   - So `gateState.head..gateState.tree` is the diff of exactly that dirty commit. The tree is the `git add -A` tree minus `.nexus`: untracked files are included and ignored files are excluded.
   - I walked clean/dirty × squash/no-squash, already-ahead and target-advanced, and each picks the right base. Detached HEAD is refused earlier.
   - Two edge cases do not block this:
     - A dirty change touching only a submodule or ignored files gives an empty diff, but the merge then commits nothing, so the message is unused.
     - A dirty squash merge whose edit exactly reverts the earlier commits gets an empty whole-branch diff while the dirty commit still takes the message. That was already the case before this delta.
2. **Tests:**
   - **Base:** On the 06ebad25 source with the new tests, `worktrunk_ai_message` fails the new no-squash test (20 pass, 1 fail).
   - **Head:** `worktrunk_ai_message`, merge, receipt, review, core and `slash_router` give 162 tests with 161 passing.
   - **The one failure** is `slash_router` "! runs a shell command". It is a Rust core handshake failure from the unbuilt core, and it fails the same way on base, so it is not a regression.
   - **Mutations that go red:** `&amp;&amp; false`, always using head as the base, and `command.ts` not passing `mergeOptions`. Each fails one test.
   - **Mutation that stays green:** dropping the `state.total &gt; 0` guard (clean branch, `--no-squash`) passes. That guard is untested; a clean one-commit-ahead `--no-squash --ai-message` test would pin it. This is a nit.
   - **Real behaviour:** The tests run the real `mergeWorktree` on real git repos with a capturing completer, not a mock of the condition.
   - **Types:** `tsc --noEmit` is clean.
3. **Plumbing:** `command.ts` passes `mergeOptions`, whose `noSquash` comes from `parsed.flags.has("no-squash")`. Both new parameters default to `{}`, so existing callers behave as before.
4. **Entire:** `entire trail finding list` for the branch returns "No findings found.", so there are no open findings and nothing new. I did not resolve anything.

`gh pr checks 448` showed test-matrix (22) and (24) and Entire Gates still pending at review time. cloudflare-experiment passed and CodeRabbit skipped. I did not wait for the pending checks, so merge only once they are green.</result>

**Fix**
- `InteractiveRepl` no longer has its own checkpoint manager field or `checkpoints()` helper, and the duplicated rebuild logic is gone. Every use goes through `this.app.getCheckpointManager()`, which already rebuilds when the workspace changes. The assignments after `--resume`, `/cd` and a merge relocation are removed, because each already updates `config.workspace` first.

- I updated the existing `FINDINGS.md` row to name `01M73JKD50YD` and say the sidecar and REPL both delegate.

**Conversation-reset question**
I found no behaviour change, and the earlier "skips conversation reset" claim in my last report was wrong:
- Moving the session into the worktree calls `setWorkspace`, which resets the conversation anyway. So a fresh first prompt starts clean whether or not the early entry ran, and `{ resume: true }` gives the same result either way.
- A genuinely resumed transcript comes from restoring a saved binding. That sets the active worktree up front, so `enterNamedWorktreeBeforeTurn` does nothing for it and the transcript is kept, as before.
- Two new tests in `worktrunk_binding.test.ts` check this with a real agent:
  - **Fresh versus resume:** early entry and no early entry give identical conversations for a fresh prompt and for `resume: true`, and a fresh first prompt has no earlier messages.
  - **Same session:** a second prompt after entering the worktree keeps the transcript of the first.
- The existing follow-up test covers the resumed-binding side.

Only `cloudflare-experiment` 22 and 24 had concluded, both `success`. The comment (https://github.com/mikeholownych/nexus-code/pull/449#issuecomment-6779120451) says to merge only once they conclude success.

- **`enterNamedWorktreeBeforeTurn`**
  - It does nothing when the flag is absent, when the session is already inside the worktree, or in read-only/dry-run mode.
  - It shares its helpers with `run()`. After it, `run()` takes the "continuing" path, so the worktree is not entered twice.
  - I confirmed the author's claim about conversation reset: `setWorkspace` calls `resetConversation()` unconditionally. A fresh first prompt starts clean either way.
  - In the sidecar, a failure emits an error and no turn starts. Turns are serialised by `activeTurn`, so there is no race.
- **REPL failure handling, not a regression:** the `/history run`, clipboard and vision callers have no local try/catch, so a failed worktree entry there (e.g. branch checked out elsewhere) escapes the prompt loop. The same failure already threw from `run()` at those call sites, so I marked it non-blocking.
- **Mutation checks:** with the call commented out in the REPL, `worktrunk_binding` goes red. With it commented out in the sidecar, `sidecar.test` goes red on both the reused-worktree and created-worktree tests. Both mutations are reverted.
- **REPL delegation:** repl.ts has no remaining direct checkpoint-manager field or import; it all goes through `app.getCheckpointManager()`. The rewired tests changed only their fixture wiring, not their assertions.

Below are the actual prompts the Entire Agents run:

Code Review:


<pasted_content id="2765">
Find the defects this Change introduces that its author would want to fix before merging: bugs, regressions, broken invariants, data loss, and missing guards on paths the Change adds or alters.

## How to review

1. Read the repository's review rules: REVIEW.md, AGENTS.md and CLAUDE.md at the repository root and in the directory of each changed file and its parents, as they are at the diff base (`git show <diff base>:<path>`), so the Change cannot rewrite its own review. The nearer file wins on conflict. A finding that rests on a rule quotes the rule and cites its file and line.
2. Read the whole diff, then trace each changed path as a running system: its callers, the changed code, and what it calls. Look in particular for:
   - Sequencing and partial failure: state left inconsistent, orphaned or lost when a later step fails or returns early; writes made before a fallible call; cleanup that runs only on success.
   - Assumptions about external or asynchronous state: how another system behaves, or a value that can change between the check and its use.
   - Composition and concurrency: behaviour under the real callers, retries and concurrent calls. Report a race only with an interleaving those callers can produce.
   - Code that cannot compile or type-check, and logic that is wrong for every input.
3. Keep going after the first defect until you have covered the whole diff.
4. Before you report a candidate, try to disprove it by reading the code: open the code it depends on, find the caller or input that triggers it, and check whether a guard elsewhere already prevents it. Drop it if you cannot show the path from the code.

Inspect with read-only commands (git, rg, reading files). Do not install dependencies or run builds or tests.

## What to report

Report a defect only when all of these hold:
- This Change introduced it. Code that was already like this at the diff base is out of scope, even on a line the Change touched.
- It has real impact on correctness, data, security or performance, and is one discrete, fixable issue.
- You can name the input, state or sequence that triggers it. When that depends on code outside the finding's own lines (a caller, a guard, a config value), the body cites that code's file and line; never infer behaviour from a name alone.
- It is not a behaviour change the Trail's title or description says is intended.
- Fixing it asks no more rigor than the surrounding code holds itself to, and the author would fix it if they knew.

Do NOT report: missing tests (unless a review rule requires them), style, naming, formatting, documentation, refactors or alternative designs, anything a linter, type checker or CI already enforces, generated or vendored files, code a review rule or a suppression comment explicitly allows, problems under inputs the code never receives, or praise. Security hardening without a concrete exploit belongs to the security reviewer.

## How to write findings

- One finding per distinct defect. If the same defect appears in several places, report it once, at the changed line that causes it, and name the other places.
- Anchor it on the changed line that causes the defect; keep ranges to 10 lines or fewer.
- Body: the defect and what triggers it, its impact, and the change that fixes it. One short paragraph, at most 3 lines of code, matter-of-fact.
- Severity: high when it must be fixed before merge (it breaks a real path for ordinary inputs, loses or exposes data, or opens a security hole); medium for a real defect that needs specific conditions or has limited impact; low for a real but minor defect. Never overstate it.
- Confidence: 0.9 or more when you traced the triggering path in the code; 0.7 to 0.9 with strong evidence and one assumption left; 0.5 to 0.7 when one condition could not be confirmed. Do not report anything below 0.5.
- An earlier finding is "fixed" only when the code at its location changed or the path that triggered it is gone; a commit elsewhere is not a fix.

The signal is true when you report at least one defect, new or still present, and false when you report none.
</pasted_content id="2765">


Security Review:


<pasted_content id="2765">
Review this change for security vulnerabilities it introduces or exposes: missing or bypassable authentication and authorization checks, privilege escalation and cross-tenant access; injection (SQL, shell command, path traversal, template); unsafe deserialization; secrets or credentials in code, logs or errors; insecure defaults; missing validation of untrusted input at a trust boundary; and exposure of data to callers who should not see it.

Trace untrusted input from where it enters to where it is used. Report only issues with a plausible exploit path through the changed code, and state the impact.

Do NOT report: general code quality, style, missing tests, or hardening suggestions without a concrete vulnerability.

The signal is true when you report at least one vulnerability, false when you report none.
</pasted_content id="2765">


Slop:


<pasted_content id="2765">
You are a very strict reviewer of agent-generated code slop. Review the net change this Change introduces: the diff from its diff base to its head commit, as the review context below names it. Judge the code, not who wrote it.

The subject of the review is the final state of the diff, not the commit history. Ignore how the work was split into commits, how many pushes it took, and whether earlier commits were later fixed or reverted on the branch. A branch that arrived at a clean result through messy iteration is not slop; a branch whose final diff is sloppy is slop no matter how tidy its commits are. Anything already at the diff base is existing code, not the subject of the review.

Work from the review context:

1. Start with the changed files and the patch in the review context. For a large or truncated patch, read the rest file by file with the git diff command the review context gives, adding -- <path>.
2. Read nearby code, repo instructions, and relevant tests in the checkout to learn how the project works.
3. You may skim the commit log from the diff base to the head commit to understand intent, but do not grade individual commits.

Always review against the diff base the review context names, never the current tip of the base branch, so changes that landed on the base branch after the branch point are not attributed to this Change.

This review is read-only against the repo's own tooling. Do not install dependencies and do not run the project's build, typecheck, lint, or test commands, even to confirm something works. Judge whether tests prove the new behavior by reading the test files and the code they exercise together, the same way you already read the diff. If a test's assertions don't match what the change claims to do, that is itself the finding.

Do not run `entire review`, use other AI agents, or hand off the review. Do it yourself with the repo and local checks.

Rate the amount of **Slop** the change adds as Low, Medium, or Large. Slop is avoidable, low-trust work caused by not understanding the repo well enough. It creates extra bugs, review work, or maintenance work.

First understand what the change is trying to do. Follow every workflow, route, state change, and rule it touches from start to finish. Read whether the tests prove the new behavior instead of only matching the code or new text.

Base the rating on:

1. **Severity** — How much can the problem break or burden?
2. **Prevalence** — How much of the change has the problem?
3. **Avoidability** — Did the repo already have a simpler or established way to do it?

Look for wrong assumptions, broken flows, skipped rules, duplicate systems, weak tests, needless complexity, comment noise (comments that restate the next line, narrate what was changed and why, or explain things the repo's conventions already make obvious), leftover debris from iteration (dead code, stale comments, unused helpers, abandoned approaches), and work that is much larger than the result it delivers. Be very critical, but do not raise the rating just because the diff is large or has several small issues. Do not call necessary complexity slop, and do not charge the change for problems it merely sits next to.

Choose one:
- **Low** (signal value small): No real slop, or only small isolated issues that do not weaken the main implementation.
- **Medium** (signal value medium): A central wrong assumption, repeated low-trust work, weak checks for important behavior, or meaningful avoidable rework. The implementation can still be fixed without replacing its core.
- **Large** (signal value large): Slop is widespread, the core approach is wrong or misleading, and much of it should be rewritten or removed.

Report the rating as the signal. Its rationale must name the two strongest reasons for the rating, using concrete behavior or architecture problems, not surface-level signals. Report each concrete slop problem behind the rating as a finding anchored to the changed code it concerns.
</pasted_content id="2765">

<result>Pushed `0f7d56ae` once to `v1/v114-wp1318-run-worktree-branch`. Entire has not re-reviewed it yet, so its list below is stale. I did not merge, resolve, dismiss or approve anything.

**Fix for `01M74NCATBMV`**
- `isTaskWorktree` in `cli/src/worktrunk/core.ts` now decides what counts as a task worktree. `kindOf` and merge's `isSpeculativeEntry` both call it.
- It requires a branch starting `nexus/` and a path that is not in the Nexus registry. The path must also match one of the shapes Nexus creates:
  - `.nexus/worktrees/&lt;id&gt;` for a task (branch `nexus/&lt;id&gt;`);
  - `.nexus/worktrees/op-&lt;key&gt;/integration`;
  - `.nexus/worktrees/op-&lt;key&gt;/units/&lt;dir&gt;` for an operation unit, including a restarted one whose branch carries a suffix.

<summary>Agent "Independent review of PR #476" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>**Verdict: ACCEPT at a9109a92.** I found no defect that Entire's Code or Security reviewers should flag. I read the diff against merge-base ace3d57a. I did not build, run or install anything.

I read AGENTS.md at the diff base. It requires XERJ reference coding in the PR body, and I could not verify that from the git tree. There is no REVIEW.md or CLAUDE.md at the base.

### Blockers
No blockers found.

### Suggestions (all low severity, none blocks merge)

- **`cli/src/repl.ts:368` (low, performance).**
  - **Trigger:** `printStatus()` now calls `surfaceFacts()` on every header print, including the non-verbose startup and clear paths.
  - **Cost:** `surfaceFacts()` calls `getContextBudget()`, which runs `calculateContextBudget` over all messages and `JSON.stringify`s the tool schemas. Only the verbose `/status` path uses the result.
  - **Fix:** compute `facts` only when `verbose` is true. For the non-verbose path, read `getApprovalMode()` and the session id directly.

- **`cli/src/repl.ts:371-374` (low, consistency).**
  - **Trigger:** `printStatus` mixes sources. Line 371 reads the mode from `facts.mode`, which comes from the agent. Line 374's MODEL suffix still reads `this.approvalMode`. The RUNTIME line still prints `this.config.endpoint`.
  - **Impact:** the two can differ if the agent's mode is changed by something other than the REPL's own `/mode` handling. One example is the checkpoint read-only fallback, which only changes the agent. The suffix would then contradict the `mode:` line in the same output.
  - **Fix:** use `facts.mode` for the suffix as well.

- **`core/src/bin/nexus_tui/protocol.rs:179-186` (low, robustness).**
  - **Trigger:** `context_tokens` and `context_limit` are `Option&lt;u64&gt;`. A fractional `contextLength` advertised by a provider would make serde reject the whole `session.state` event, not just that field. `resolveLimits` passes `advertised.contextLength` through unrounded (`provider_capabilities.ts:160-167`).
  - **Likelihood:** this is unlikely. `totalActiveTokens` is always an integer (`tokens.ts:73-83`, and `Math.ceil` on the scaled path).
  - **Fix:** apply `Math.floor` to `context_limit` in `surfaceFacts`.

- **`cli/test/surface_agreement.test.ts:56-86` (low, test strength).**
  - **Trigger:** both surfaces read `surfaceFacts()`, so the field-by-field comparison cannot fail on drift between them. A mistake in `surfaceFacts` itself would pass.
  - **Mitigation:** the literal assertions at lines 80-86 (`mode` is `read-only`, `read_only` is `yes`, tokens are greater than 0, the model string, and the endpoint kind) do pin the real values.
  - **Fix:** none required. Optionally add a case where `readOnly` is false and the mode is `review` or `auto`, so a hardcoded default would also fail.

- **`docs/v1.1.4/FINDINGS.md:173` (low, documentation).** I-3 is marked FIXED. The TUI half is covered by two Rust unit tests (`protocol::tests::parses_every_rendered_event_type` and the `live_view` status bar test), not by a TUI-to-sidecar test. The row itself says no PTY capture test exists, so this is disclosed.

### Slop rating: Low
1. The change reuses one shared `surfaceFacts()` helper and adds no duplicate systems. It also does the right thing on the wire: the four new fields are optional in `protocol.ts` and `#[serde(default)]` in Rust, so an older sidecar still parses. The `protocol.ts` change adds only comment lines.
2. The only issues are the unconditional budget computation in the non-verbose REPL header and the mixed mode sources in that function. Both are isolated and do not weaken the core.

### Security
- The four new fields are a boolean, two integers and a closed `"local"|"cloud"` enum. `endpoint_kind` comes from `disclosureClass()`, not from user text.
- No new trust boundary or secret exposure. The sidecar's event field-length and redaction handling is untouched.
- The cloud-versus-local disclosure now also reaches the TUI, which is the intended direction.

### Good
- The `surfaceFacts` helper removes the duplicated `getApprovalMode()` and model-string logic in the sidecar. The `calls === 2` adjustment in `cli/test/sidecar.test.ts` correctly follows from that.
- The status bar keeps `read-only` at priority 1, next to the mode, so it survives at 40 columns, and drops context and endpoint kind first. That behaviour has a width-bound test.
- The tests cover both the old-sidecar case (no invented values) and a provider session where the model reads `provider · model`.

<result>Both PRs are pushed. All CI is still pending, so neither is verified green yet, and I have not re-listed the Entire findings on either branch.

**#469** (head `8e14b26014a77afe02b1a358812ef6488f98ef5d`)
- **CI so far:** CodeQL, javascript-typescript, both cloudflare-experiment jobs and CodeRabbit pass. Entire Gates and test-matrix (22 and 24) are still pending. My background watch on this PR has not reported anything yet.
- **Merge:** I merged origin/main and resolved the two doc conflicts.
  - Every FINDINGS.md row survives. Main's D-04 section stays as J, and this PR's follow-ups are renamed K.
  - TUI-AUDIT-STATUS.md keeps both the D-01 and D-04 rows, and its D-01 row now notes the marker and short-terminal behaviour.
  - Main's status-bar behaviour from #476 is untouched.
- **Finding 01M7202ZPHQV (medium):** a short terminal could report a multiline command as fully read when its middle rows were never shown.
  - **Marker row:** the fold marker now has its own row and no longer overwrites a command row. A panel with no room for a command row plus the marker shows "enlarge the terminal" and cannot approve.
  - **Read state:** the command only counts as read when the frame that showed it was actually drawn.
  - **Notice row:** a refusal notice no longer takes the only content row on a short terminal. This hole appeared while testing the other two.
- **Finding 01M71ZXYHGQY (low):** I removed the unused `now` variable and the `let _ = now;` line.
- **Test:** `y_approves_a_long_command_only_after_every_row_of_it_was_on_screen` sweeps widths 40 and 60 and heights 10 to 26, with a short and a long subject. It checks every command row appeared on screen before `y` approves, and that no row exceeds the terminal width. It failed before the fix and passes now.
- **Review:** the pre-push review found a new bug in my first fix. An unsigned subtraction could underflow, and in a release build `y` could then approve a command with no rows shown. I fixed it with a saturating subtraction and checked the sweep panics without it. The one-line fix was not re-reviewed.

## Open Entire findings
- **Medium, `cli/src/core/egress.ts:548`:** an extension that moves a `/` into the path exemption makes the scanner reject the whole span and skip the key inside it. Input: `sk-cache.sk-Qx7Km2Vb9LrTz4/Models`. The base redacted the inner key; the head leaves it visible. Confidence 0.98.
- **Medium, `cli/src/core/egress.ts:1077`:** extending a deterministic match discards the original match. Input: `REDACTED.Xo2RtBq; saved REDACTED.log`. The second complete key now stays in clear.
- **Low, `cli/test/redactor_encoded_dotted.test.ts:88-91`:** the decoded-length test has a wrong comment, and its assertion cannot fail, so it does not prove the gate change.

The first head had 2 open findings, both Low. I fixed both in round 1:
- The entropy gates now use the decoded length.
- I corrected the JWT/JWE comment.

Both new Medium findings are regressions I introduced with the dotted-extension fix. Both come from replacing or advancing past the original match.

## What changed (`cli/src/core/egress.ts`, the shared redactor)
- **Encoded slash and plus:** detection normalises `%2F`, `%2B`, `%252F` and the look-alikes U+2215, U+2044, U+FF0F and U+FF0B. The redacted span is always the original text. This covers the `sk-` scanner and the entropy tokenizer.
- **Dotted `sk-` continuation:** a dot followed by key material (6 or more characters, with a digit, `/` or `+`, or not splitting into words) continues the key. Sentence full stops, `.json`, `.Models`, `.toString` and `.README` end the key as before. The same continuation applies to ghp_/github_pat_, AIza, hf_, xox and JWT.
- **Dotted entropy tokens:** `isDottedKeyMaterial` accepts only strict dotted tokens. It has no `/`, and each segment is 6 or more characters with a digit, a capital after the first character, or `+`. At least one segment is 16 or more characters that do not split into words.
- **FINDINGS.md:** the two `PR #440 review` rows are marked FIXED with test names, and no other rows were touched.

## Tests
- **New file:** `cli/test/redactor_encoded_dotted.test.ts`, red at origin/main for the fix cases and green now.
- **Negatives:** versions, file, host and class names, URLs, GGUF names, `%2F` inside paths, a base64 data URI, sentence ends, and `.Models`/`.toString` tails keep their current behaviour.
- **ReDoS:** long adversarial inputs under a time bound, plus a scaling check on chains of dotted keys.
- **Wider run:** `tsc --noEmit` is clean. The 27 redaction/egress suites show 61 failures, the same set as at origin/main, all host-environment failures. No new failures, so no new false-positive regressions on them.

## Residual risks and what I left
- **Short tails:** key tails under 6 characters, or with no digit/`/`/`+` and not word-splitting, stay in clear after a dot.
- **Other encodings:** `%3D` padding and other percent-encodings are not handled.
- **Generic dotted tokens with `/`:** not redacted by the entropy path.
- **AKIA ids:** not extended, because they are identifiers rather than secrets.
- **Literal patterns:** the `ui.ts` and `SecretsVault` patterns are unchanged.

## Suggested next step
Fix all three together. For the two Mediums:
- Keep the original match alongside the extended span.
- When an extended candidate is rejected, resume scanning for `sk-` starts inside it instead of skipping the whole run.
- Add the two inputs above as tests.

For the Low, use an encoded token that is under 20 characters decoded but would pass the entropy threshold, and correct the character counts in the comment.

I'd like your go-ahead before spending that round, since it exceeds the stop condition you set.</result>

## What changed this round
- **Original matches are kept.** A dotted or encoded extension is now emitted as an additional value, never a replacement. So `REDACTED` is still replaced on its own elsewhere in the text.
- **Rejected extensions no longer hide inner keys.** The scanner now resumes after the original run, so `sk-cache.sk-Qx7Km2Vb9LrTz4/Models` finds the inner key again.
- **Extensions have a budget.** A per-call `ExtensionBudget` bounds both the scan work and the length of extension values. Without it, a crafted input produced 8.2 MB of values from 120 KB of text; it now produces about 121 KB.
- **Partial overlaps are replaced whole.** `replaceValues` now falls back to the union replacement when an occurrence only partly overlaps text already replaced. This fixes a value straddling the end of an extension leaving its tail in clear. It deliberately relaxes the old "identical to sequential `replaceAll`" contract: the result now matches it only when no two values overlap partly. I rewrote the pinned test in `redactor_slash_plus.test.ts` to match (nested and disjoint cases still equal sequential exactly), so please confirm you accept that contract change. I tried adding a union value in `resolveOverlaps` first, but the local review showed it breaks monotonicity, so I reverted it.
- **More look-alikes.** U+29F8 and U+FE62 are now handled too.
- **Low finding.** The wrong character count in the test comment is corrected, and the new test uses a 19-character decoded token that passes the entropy threshold.

## Tests
- **Red first at the previous head:**
  - `sk-cache.sk-Qx7Km2Vb9LrTz4/Models`
  - `REDACTED.Xo2RtBq; saved REDACTED.log`
  - the monotonicity test, which failed on 289 ranges
- **Monotonicity test:** it compares redacted character ranges from `replaceValues` against a frozen record of what origin/main redacted over 2,988 generated inputs. The record is guarded by a count and a digest, and `cli/test/fixtures/record_redaction_base_spans.ts` regenerates it. The corpus uses no operator secrets; those are covered by separate tests.
- **Other new tests:** operator secrets that straddle a key or each other, the extension-size budget, and adversarial timing inputs.
- **Wider run:** `tsc --noEmit` is clean. The 27 related suites show 61 failures, the same set as origin/main (host-environment failures), so no new ones.
- **Local reviews:** I ran the Code, Security and Slop reviews at each new head before pushing. The final one rated Slop Low and found no security issue. I fixed its remaining items, but did not re-review the last small edits.

## Residual risks
- Extensions are skipped once the per-call budget is spent. An attacker can exhaust it to leave a dotted tail unextended, but that is never worse than origin/main.
- Operator secrets are matched at their first occurrence only (existing behaviour), so a later occurrence that straddles an extension is not unioned.
- Not handled: `\u002F` escapes, HTML entities, triple percent-encoding, `%3D` padding, and key tails under 6 characters or lowercase-only after a dot.
- The frozen fixture is 62 KB of one-line JSON. It is reproducible only with the recorder script and a copy of the base file.

Main file: `cli/src/core/egress.ts`. Tests: `cli/test/redactor_encoded_dotted.test.ts` and `cli/test/redactor_slash_plus.test.ts`.</result>

<summary>Agent "Independent security review of PR #478" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>**Verdict: ACCEPT** at head `ce51ee81880f7c25ea93a385011485e66e7e6876`. I confirmed that `origin/v1/v114-redaction-encoded-keys` resolves to this SHA. The diff base is `29e7ebff9a6f171438115d8a404a5f18a3fe4bc1`. Review was by reading only; I did not run or build anything.

**Slop: Low.**

### Blockers
No blockers found.

### Findings (none blocking, all low)

1. **Low, `cli/src/core/egress.ts:538-546, 590-591, 1091, 1133-1136`.** An attacker can use up the extension budget so that the new encoded and dotted detection stops working later in the same string.
   - Trigger: a leading `sk-&lt;20 chars&gt;` followed by `.abcdef1` repeated about 128 times. Several scans charge the same bytes: `scanBase64Keys`, the OPENAI pattern, and the ANTHROPIC pattern for `sk-ant-` keys. A real `sk-…%2F…` key later in the string then falls back to its pre-PR shape.
   - This is not worse than origin/main. I traced budget exhaustion: `runEnd` and the original pattern `end` are always emitted, because `limit` collapses to the start and `chargeExtension` returns `from`. The decoded entropy-token path (`isDottedKeyMaterial`) is not budgeted and still covers `%2F` tokens.
   - Fix, optional: charge each distinct extension span once. For example, memoise `extendDottedKey` per `end`, or cache the result in `extensionBudget` so the OPENAI and ANTHROPIC passes share it. Add a test where a large dotted blob precedes a real encoded key and the key must still be redacted fully.

2. **Low, `cli/src/core/egress.ts:538-545`.** When the limit cuts a very long key-material segment, the extension ends at the limit and leaves the rest of the segment in clear.
   - Trigger: `ghp_…` followed by `.` and more than about 1024 base64 characters, or a nearly spent budget. The main-branch outcome was a full leak of the tail, so this is not a regression.

3. **Low, test design, `cli/test/redactor_encoded_dotted.test.ts` and `cli/test/fixtures/record_redaction_base_spans.ts`.** The frozen fixture is only loosely pinned.
   - The digest covers the corpus inputs only. `redaction_base_spans.json` itself is free-form, and the recorder takes any file path as the base. Regenerating it from head code would pass the monotone test even if redaction had regressed, because only reviewer discipline guards the base copy.
   - Fix: store the sha256 of the `29e7ebff` `egress.ts` source in the JSON and have the recorder refuse to write unless its base file hashes to that value. The test can then assert the recorded value is the pinned constant. Both the recorder and the test are in this PR.
   - The corpus has no explicit operator secrets, no budget-exhausted input, and no multi-occurrence inputs. The monotone claim therefore does not cover finding 1's path.

4. **Low, over-redaction, `cli/src/core/egress.ts:520-522`.** `isKeyMaterialSegment` treats any segment of 6+ characters that does not split into dictionary-style words as key material.
   - Trigger: a sentence like `…sk-&lt;key&gt;.x-request-id`, or `.Release2024` with no space after the full stop. The following word is redacted along with the key. This only happens directly after a recognised key, so it is an accepted trade-off. It is worth noting in the docs.

### Disproved candidates

- **Monotonicity of the classifier.**
  - Every original match is still pushed: `runEnd`, the pattern `end`, and the original `secretVal` are kept, and extensions are additional matches.
  - `normalizeKeyChars` is a no-op on any run or token that main could have matched. Main only matched tokens made of `[a-zA-Z0-9+/=_-]`, which contain no `%` and no look-alikes. The decoded `length` equals `tok.length` unless an encoded character is present.
  - The `resolveOverlaps` drop rule cannot newly drop a token main kept. A new deterministic match with the same start either ends past the token (the token cannot contain `%` or `.`) or was already dominated by the original match.
- **Monotonicity of `replaceValues`.**
  - Fully-inside occurrences are skipped, which is sound for a union of accepted spans.
  - Partial overlaps go to `replaceUnion`, which is a superset of every occurrence. Main left tails there, so this closes a leak rather than opening one.
  - Operator secrets are handled the same way.
  - In reversible mode, the token is keyed to `matched`, so rehydration restores the original region.
  - The `patternName` of a merged region is that of the longest value, which is cosmetic.
- **ReDoS and quadratic cost.**
  - Every new regex is linear. The sticky `ENCODED_KEY_CHAR` has no nested quantifiers. The `lastIndex` state is set before each use.
  - Scans are bounded by `min(MAX_EXTENSION, budget.left)`. Dotted segments cannot overlap the next key's segment, because a key with a continuation needs a `.` after it, and that `.` ends the preceding segment.
  - Entropy-path `isDottedKeyMaterial` only runs on tokens of at most 128 characters, since `/` is excluded from segments.
- **Offset mapping.**
  - All look-alike characters are single BMP code units, so surrogate pairs and combining characters are not an issue.
  - Detection runs on a normalised copy, but all spans and secrets are sliced from the original text. Decoded lengths are used only for gates and entropy.
  - `%25252F` (triple encoding) is not decoded, which matches the stated scope.
- **False positives.** The negative corpus (versions, hosts, GGUF names, URLs, base64 images, Maven names, `sk-learn`) holds up against `isKeyMaterialSegment`'s 6-character minimum. Dotted entropy tokens need every segment to be key material and one segment of 16+ characters with entropy of at least 4.0.
- **Tests.**
  - The new assertions match the claimed behaviour: the 19- versus 20-character gate, the straddling operator secret, and the budget-size bound.
  - The operator-overlap test would fail on main.
  - The existing random replace test is correctly relaxed through `hasPartialOverlap`.
  - Wall-clock bounds are loose (5 s).

### Debris
- Several doc comments were reflowed unevenly, with very long lines in the `replaceValues` doc and the `scanBase64Keys` docblock. The session-id comment was re-wrapped without a content change. This is cosmetic.
- The 63 KB single-line `redaction_base_spans.json` fixture is unavoidable for a coverage-level test.
- The recorder script under `fixtures/` is not run by `run_tests.mjs`, which only picks up `*.test.ts`. It is dead unless invoked by hand, which is intended.

### Good
- The "original match is never replaced, only supplemented" design is applied consistently in three places. These are `scanBase64Keys`, the deterministic-pattern loop, and the `Set` of candidate ends.
- A coverage-level monotonicity test against recorded origin/main spans, over about 2,988 generated shapes including joiner, wrapper and chained-key combinations, is the right kind of regression guard for a shared redactor.
- The extension budget bounds both scan work and value size, with a test asserting a total-size cap.
- `replaceValues` now fails safe on partial overlaps instead of leaving tails in clear.

- `/home/mike/nexus-wt/v114-redact/cli/test/redactor_slash_plus.test.ts`

<result>**Verdict: ACCEPT** at head `d199dfaeb1efac37713e8feaf32c0834d60ba7df`.

I confirmed `origin/v1/v114-wp1317-visual-polish` is that SHA. The diff base is `29e7ebff9a6f171438115d8a404a5f18a3fe4bc1`, which is also the current `origin/main` tip. I read the code only; I ran nothing.

## Code Review
No blockers and no medium defects. Four low findings:

1. **Low, `core/src/bin/nexus_tui/live.rs:357-365` (also `live_state.rs:1032`).** The breadcrumb prints `session_id` with a raw `println!` and an unquoted `nexus --resume {id}`.
   - Trigger: `/save &lt;arg&gt;` (`cli/src/slash/session_commands.ts:220`) stores a user-typed string as the session id. An arg with spaces, `;` or escape bytes is echoed verbatim to the terminal and is unsafe to paste.
   - It is user-typed, so there is no cross-trust exploit.
   - Fix: pass the id through `diff_view::safe_text`, and skip the breadcrumb or quote the id when it is not `[A-Za-z0-9_.-]+`.

2. **Low, `live_state.rs:967` (`session_saved |= saved`).** The flag is a bare bool, not tied to a session id.
   - Trigger: turn 1 saves, then turn 2's `saveSession` throws. `turn.result` carries `saved:false`, but the flag stays true and the breadcrumb still prints.
   - The earlier record, or a progress-listener write, exists on disk, so the id is resumable. The text "saved" is just stale.
   - Fix: acceptable as is, or store `saved_session: Option&lt;String&gt;` and compare it with `session.session_id` at print time. That also makes the `/save &lt;id&gt;` rename case structurally safe.

3. **Low, `cli/src/sidecar.ts:647-652` with `saveSession` at line 923.** `saveSession` returns silently when `messages.length &lt;= 1`, so `saved` stays `true` although nothing was written.
   - I found no path to it. The turn always appends the user message before `runTurn`, and the early-return paths emit `error`, not `turn.result`.
   - Fix: have `saveSession` return a boolean and set `saved = this.saveSession(...)`.

4. **Low, `live_state.rs:1216`.** After a crash and `r`, quitting without a completed turn prints no breadcrumb, even though the old session is on disk and resumable. This is safe and conservative, and it matches the disclosed intent.

**(c) Every `turn.result` path.** There is exactly one emitter, `sidecar.ts:662`.
- Success, failure (`failedTurnFromError`), and mid-turn cancel all flow through the same `saved` computation, and the write-failure catch sets `false`.
- The pre-turn cancel and checkpoint-decline paths emit `error` only, so no `saved` is needed.
- A restarted sidecar is a fresh process with the same code.
- Rust reads a missing `saved` as `true` via `#[serde(default = "turn_saved_by_default")]` (`protocol.rs:157`). The sidecar always emits it now, so the default only matters for old sidecars. `protocol.rs:659-662` tests both the default and the `false` case.
- The existing TS tests only use `.find` and `.map` on events, so the extra field breaks no exact-match assertion.

**(d) Breadcrumb integrity.** I found no way to advertise an unsaved or stale id.
- `restarted()` clears the flag, and the new id is set by the next `session.state`.
- The flag becomes true only on:
  - a `turn.result` with `saved`;
  - `/resume` success, which implies the record loaded;
  - a non-empty `history`.
- `/clear` keeps the id and does not reset the flag. The prior saved record still exists, so the hint is accurate.
- The breadcrumb prints after `drop(guard)` and after `Action::Quit`, so the terminal is already restored.

**(e) Narrow terminals.**
- `render_models` clamps with `saturating_add` and `clamp(8, 24)`. `modal()` takes `min(area.height, wanted)`.
- The `inner.height &lt; 3` early return guards `inner.y + 2` and `bottom() - 1`.
- The orientation card is gated on height at least 12 and width at least 50, and `Paragraph::line_count` handles wrapping and clips to `area.height`.
- The markdown fence row width is unchanged at `width - 1`: the lead is 3 columns, `room` dropped by 1, and the marker moved into the content column.
- The diff does not touch the approval panel, so no approval-relevant row can be cut by it.

**(f) Overlap with #476 and #469.**
- The `live_view.rs` hunks are only the welcome card (around line 1189) and `render_models`. `render_status` is untouched.
- The `live_state.rs` hunks are additive: `TurnResult` (one line after `cancel_started`), `slash.result` resume, `History`, and `restarted`. Approval handling is untouched.
- Expect only textual merge conflicts near `TurnResult`/`close_approval` and the `Event::TurnResult` pattern. No logical overlap.

## Security Review
No vulnerabilities with an exploit path. The only item is the unsanitized id print (finding 1), which is self-inflicted. No new input crosses a trust boundary, and `saved` is a plain boolean.

## Author-disclosed gaps
- **(a) Unreviewed last commits.** Reviewed here; nothing beyond the findings above.
- **(b) Newest tests.**
  - `the_model_panel_keeps_its_height_while_a_filter_is_typed` is a real regression guard. `models.rs:259` pushes typed characters into `query`, and `zzzz` drops `rows()` to 0, so a height of `rows().len()+5` would go from 11 to 8 and fail. At the diff base the modal was full height, so it would pass there. It is red only against the intermediate `rows()`-based sizing. Only the modal's `╰` and the title match the border filter, so the measurement is sound.
  - The `saved:false` unit tests in `live.rs` and `protocol.rs` are genuine: without the `|=` guard, `saved:false` would flip the flag.

## Suggestions (not blockers)
- No TypeScript test pins `saved:false`. Add a test in `cli/test/sidecar_surface.test.ts` that stubs `saveSession` to throw and asserts `turn.result.saved === false` alongside the notice.
- Neither `docs/v1.1.4/FINDINGS.md` nor `TUI-AUDIT-STATUS.md` is touched by this PR, although WP-1317 closes rows there. Update them, or state in the PR that the rows are deferred.
- `model_picker_on_a_40x10_terminal_does_not_panic` asserts only `!rendered.is_empty()`, and base did not panic either. Assert something meaningful, such as the "Select model" title or that a row height of at most 10 fits.
- `model_picker_modal_height_is_clamped_proportionally_to_content` recomputes the implementation formula (a tautology). The `== 11` pin is the useful part; drop the formula.

## Slop: Low
- **Reason 1:** a few weak or duplicated tests. The 40x10 test is vacuous. The `session_saved` unit test in `live_state.rs` overlaps the three breadcrumb tests in `live.rs`. The height test restates the production formula.
- **Reason 2:** minor comment noise in the tests ("When a turn completes:", "When resuming with history:") and a doc comment that restates the field name. The core approach is sound and proportionate, and it fixes the earlier rounds' root causes: the `restarted()` reset, the protocol-level `saved`, and `unfiltered_len` for stable sizing.

## Good
- Putting `saved` in the protocol, with a backward-compatible serde default and a test, is the right fix over inferring it client-side.
- `unfiltered_len` gives the picker one sizing source and `rows_matching` removes duplication.
- Conservative fail-closed breadcrumb: when unsure, print nothing.
- The saturating and clamp arithmetic in the modal path.

<result>**Verdict: REQUEST_CHANGES** at head `fc1c7ad849a4fcf4650c5d7a889d43f7c4dfbee6`.

I confirmed `origin/v1/v114-wp1317-d01-approval` equals that SHA. The diff base is `29e7ebff`, which is #476, and `origin/main` is an ancestor of the head. I only read code, so none of this was run or built.

## Code Review and Security Review

### Blockers

- **High, `core/src/bin/nexus_tui/live_state.rs:1351` (`read_all`), `:1572` (`End =&gt; usize::MAX`).** The "read every row" rule is not implemented. `read_all` is only `scroll &gt;= overlay_limit`, and no record is kept of which rows were drawn.
  - Trigger: `run_command` with a 60-row script, the malicious rows in the middle, a terminal showing about 6 rows. Press `End`, then `y`. The middle rows were never on screen, and `y` approves.
  - The marker text says "↓ or End to scroll", which advertises the skip. The manual and FINDINGS K say "scrolled to its end", so the docs match the code. They do not match the stated rule that y approves only after every row was on screen.
  - Fix: add `Approval.seen_through: usize`. In `note_layout`, when `layout.approval_drawn` and `top &lt;= seen_through`, set `seen_through = max(seen_through, top + viewport)`. Set `read_all = seen_through &gt;= lines.len()`.
    - This needs the drawn `top` and `viewport` exposed through `LiveLayout`.
    - Reset `seen_through` when the width changes, since wrapped row counts change.
    - Update the notice, marker, manual and FINDINGS wording to match.
  - Add a test: `End` first, then `y`, must refuse.

- **Medium, `core/src/bin/nexus_tui/review.rs` (the `Change` and `Patch` branches of `review_lines`) and `live_view.rs:665`.** Sanitising is incomplete, although the stated claim is that all rows are sanitised.
  - `safe_row` is only applied to the `Shell`, `Verifier` and `Args` branches.
  - `markdown::wrap` does not sanitise. `safe_text` is called only in `inline`, `markdown.rs:151`.
  - Trigger: `write_file` creating an empty file, or an identical overwrite, at a path containing ESC or U+202E. `unified` yields no hunks, so `patch` is `None` and the raw text path draws `new file {path}`, `--- {path}`, `+++ {path}` and `{path}: current content not shown…` with raw bytes. A `Patch` review whose text has no parsable hunk draws `patch {path}` and every `classify` row the same way.
  - Impact: terminal escape injection (OSC 52, cursor movement) and RLO spoofing inside the approval panel.
  - Fix: run `safe_row` on every `ReviewLine` text in `review_lines`, for example by wrapping `line()` for the Meta, Hunk and Context kinds. Alternatively call `diff_view::safe_text` at `live_view.rs:665`. Also sanitise `tool_verb(&amp;approval.tool)` at `live_view.rs:577`, because the unknown-tool fallback returns the raw name (`live_view.rs:1897`–`1920`).
  - Add a test: an empty-file create with an ESC path must not put raw ESC or U+202E in the approval rows.

### Suggestions

- **Low, `live.rs` event loop (burst handling).** `burst_may_answer` counts only pasteable keys. A machine-speed stream such as Left then `y`, or `y` followed by an arrow or Ctrl chord, is handled one event at a time. The non-pasteable event is stashed in `pending` and `keys.len()` is 1, so `y` answers. Left sets neither `last_text` nor `last_key`, and the opening guard applies only at panel open.
  - Fix: when an approval is open, count every `Key Press` in the drained queue toward the burst. Also treat a `y`/`n`/`a`/Enter that arrives with more events pending as a burst.
- **Low, `diff_view.rs:408`.** `visible_control` omits other characters that are invisible or look like spaces:
  - U+FE00–FE0F and U+E0100–E01EF (variation selectors);
  - U+00A0, U+2000–200A, U+202F, U+205F, U+3000 and U+1680 (space look-alikes);
  - U+FFF9–FFFB and U+180B–180D.
  - NBSP in `rm\u00a0-rf` shows as a space but is not one in bash. Fix: add these ranges and extend the test list.
- **Low, FINDINGS K row 2.** This is disclosed and open: Approve on a long diff is not gated, and `folded` is false when `patch` is `Some`, so a diff can be cut off with no marker. Say so on the panel, or gate diffs the same way. Resolve it together with the High finding.
- **Low, `live_state.rs:264`.** `Approval.opened` is written at `:1304` and `:1390` but read only by tests. This is debris; remove it or use it.
- The doc comments on `APPROVAL_GUARD` and the other constants are long and narrate history, for example "also one ignored in a typing run, or refused on an unseen fold". Trim them.

### Checked and found sound

- **Disclosed saturating-sub fix:** I traced every subtraction in `render_approval`. `area.height.saturating_sub(2)`, `body_room`, the `viewport` computation, `limit - top` (`top &lt;= limit`) and `footer_rows &lt;= inner.height` are all safe. At 0 to 2 rows, and below `MIN_WIDTH` or `MIN_HEIGHT`, `render_live` returns a default layout. That leaves `approval_drawn` false, so `shown` is false and only Esc denies. `note_layout` also resets the approval scroll to 0 in that state, which is the safe direction.
- **Opening guard and resize:** the guard is re-derived when the panel is re-shown, and `last_key` is set by every typing key. `note_layout` runs after every draw, so `read_all` is recomputed before the next key.
- **Queued approvals:** a second approval is queued and starts with `shown=false`. A key that answered the first sets `last_key`, which arms the guard on the second.
- **Pastes and held keys:** bracketed paste goes to the draft and re-arms the guard. The 1 s confirm delay and repeat window behave as the manual describes.
- **Manual text:** it matches the code for the 0.3 s typing run, the 1 s window, the "Esc at any time" rule, and the two residual cases.
- **Docs and #476:** `FINDINGS.md` sections J and K and the `TUI-AUDIT-STATUS.md` D-01 row are present and truthful. The status bar code with `read_only`, context and endpoint kind (`live_view.rs:1164–1171`) has no hunks in this diff. Its #476 test files are not touched. I did not run that test.

## Slop rating: Low to Medium

The two strongest reasons:

- **Overstated claim.** The PR claims every row is sanitised and every row was read, but the code covers only the Shell, Verifier and Args branches, and `read_all` ignores the middle rows.
- **Comment noise.** The comments are heavy and some are stale, and there is debris in `opened`.

The tests are otherwise thorough: size sweeps, repeat-window cases and PTY end-to-end. If the two blockers are fixed with tests, this drops to Low.

The earlier head `fc1c7ad8` had 1 (01M76275T0NH), so the count went up (1 to 4) and no further round is allowed. The two medium findings are real regressions I introduced in this round.

CI at this head: CodeQL, javascript-typescript, both cloudflare-experiment jobs and CodeRabbit pass. test-matrix (22), test-matrix (24) and Entire Gates were still pending when I checked. I did not need to dispatch CI by hand.

| Finding | Severity | Location | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| 01M769FG15G4FSW6SXQGSZYBQB | medium | `diff_view.rs:411` (`visible_control`) | Real, caused by my change |
| 01M769K1VC23367M2G98EMKBZP | medium | `live.rs:419` / `live.rs:522-526` | Real, caused by my change |
| 01M769FG1HVC2SG9MDS8RN4DZK | low | `live_view.rs:724` (`scrub`) | Valid, same slop my review rated Low |
| 01M769FG1CRRPJNYN47Q5MXS0K | low | `live_view.rs:4516` (test) | Valid, weak test |

**What each one is, and the fix:**

- **Widened `visible_control` (medium).**
  - That function is shared with the transcript and markdown renderers, so my widened set turns ordinary prose into `⚠&lt;U+FE0F&gt; done` and `&lt;U+3000&gt;`.
  - I extended the shared predicate when I should have added an approval-only set.
  - Fix: a separate predicate for approval and command text, used by `review_lines` and the final scrub, leaving prose on the old set.
- **Burst handling (medium).**
  - At an open approval, queued `x`, Backspace, `z` becomes a paste of `xz` followed by Backspace, which leaves `x` instead of `z`.
  - Fix: process the burst in its original order. Text keys go to the draft, never to approval decisions; editing and navigation keys act in sequence.
- **`scrub` (low).** Every source already sanitises, so this pass is redundant, and if it ever fired it would widen already-wrapped lines and clip them. Drop it, or run it before wrapping.
- **Weak test (low).** The assertion `flat.contains("ENDOFLABEL") || !flat.contains("a_section")` also passes if the label is never drawn. It should assert the full label. The U+2800 check belongs in the existing stand-in test.

The unit tests did not cover either medium: nothing exercised prose through the shared renderer or Backspace inside a burst.

The "every row on screen" fix, the sanitising choke point, the diff gating and the size sweep are not among the open findings. The first four rows of the Rust suite, 300 tests in `nexus-tui`, passed locally before the push.

**Recommendation.** One more small round, on an owner decision, is enough to clear all four:
1. Add a separate approval-only visibility predicate.
2. Make burst handling order-preserving.
3. Remove `scrub`.
4. Tighten the label test.

If the owner declines another round, #469 as pushed is a net regression on shared text rendering and should not merge as is. The regression is the prose rendering, which was fine before this round.

<result>**Verdict: ACCEPT** at head `5bc37bd0a30dbe84cc61450d02582131cfdfd64c` (verified equal to `origin/v1/v114-wp1308-harness-provenance`; diff base `29e7ebff`). I read the code and tests but did not run anything. No blockers, no medium or high findings, and three Low suggestions.

## Code Review
No defect rises above Low.

**Consumers of the old prefix.**
- `rg '\[Nexus'` over `cli/src` finds no remaining recogniser. Every hit is a producer string or a tool-result decoration.
- The four prefix-based consumers are gone: compaction's earlier task requests (`agent.ts:3333`), the current request (`:3612`), the findings request (`:3744`), and the TUI objective (`tui.ts:95`).
- The sidecar history check at `sidecar.ts:994` now uses `isHarnessNote`.
- `isHarnessNote` (`harness_notes.ts:8`) requires `role === "user"` and `harness` equal to `"note"` or `"skill-note"`. Any other value, such as a corrupted `true`, is treated as operator text.

**Producers.**
- Against `v1.1.3`, every user-role push of a Nexus note is now created through `harnessNoteMessage`: recovery (`:6423`), cut-off (`:6600`, `:7590`), parser notices (`:6718`), empty reply (`:6736`), continuation (`:6749`) and subagent recovery (`:7684`).
- The only remaining unmarked pushes are the operator prompt (`:6148`), steering (`:2801`), and two non-persisted constructions, `:2048` (admission estimate) and `:3830` (cut-response guard).
- No other `harness:` assignment exists in `cli/src`.

**Can an operator, model or tool string acquire provenance?**
- Only through the legacy migration on load. It is not called on any live path, and `setMessages` has no callers in `src`.
- Provider output and tool results never pass through `harnessNoteMessage` as message role. The sidecar protocol does not construct `ChatMessage`s.
- A hand-edited session file can set `harness` directly. That is the same trust level as the existing `"skill-note"` marker and needs local write access. It is not a new boundary.
- The marker carries no authority. A note is still a user-role message to the model, and `harness` only classifies it for compaction, the findings request and the history view.

**Legacy migration.**
- It never demotes or rewrites content. It skips any message where `harness !== undefined`, returns a new array and replaces the element with `{...message, harness:"note"}`. That makes it idempotent and non-mutating.
- It is anchored on the exact wording of each v1.1.3 note. The regexes match the real v1.1.3 producer strings and the parser notice strings.
- It also requires a position: not the first message, and after an assistant message, a tool message, a marked note or a non-first system message. Only the cut-off note may follow an unmarked user message.
- The accepted ambiguity is a v1.1.3 operator message that pastes a legacy note verbatim after a reply. I judge the tradeoff sound. The pasted text is the operator's own, in an already-saved session, and the consequence is only that the message is left out of the "Earlier task requests" list and out of the findings request. The text is still sent to the model unchanged, and nothing is hidden from the operator or gains authority.
- The FINDINGS claim that new messages are never affected holds, since migration runs on load only.

**`keptNotes` and `compactionCandidate`.**
- A trailing run of unanswered harness notes after the operator request is kept whole.
- The real operator request is now always kept verbatim as `currentUserIndex`. At base, a trailing recovery note was treated as the current user message and the real request was summarised away, so this is an improvement.
- Candidate filters and the final `selected.set` are consistent, and the order is preserved by sorting on index.

## Findings

### Suggestions (all Low)

1. **Low — `cli/src/agent.ts:3617` (the author's item 1: queued steering after an unanswered note drops the note).**
   - **Trigger:** `endStep` pushes a recovery or cut-off note, then `applyQueuedSteering` pushes steering before the next request, and compaction runs on that request.
   - **Effect:** The steering message is an unmarked user message, so it becomes `currentUserIndex` and the earlier note falls outside `keptNotes`. That note is a one-shot retry hint and gets summarised away. This is the same behaviour as base, not a regression.
   - **Loss or leak:** No operator intent is lost, because the steering message is kept and the objective is pinned. Nothing leaks.
   - **Fix:** In the loop at `:3617`, keep marked notes after the last operator request even when later unmarked user messages are steering, for example by skipping `pinnedUserText(...)` messages when walking back. Alternatively, record the limitation in FINDINGS.

2. **Low — `cli/src/harness_notes.ts:49-50` (the author's item 2: migration is not exact for compacted v1.1.3 sessions).**
   - **Trigger:** A v1.1.3 post-compaction session where a retained note sits directly after an unmarked user message, other than a cut-off note. That is a shape v1.1.3 compaction should not normally produce, since its last user message was the note itself.
   - **Effect:** The note stays unmarked and is treated as operator text, which only affects compaction's earlier-task list, the findings request and the TUI objective. Because it is user-role text either way, nothing is hidden from or leaked to the model beyond what v1.1.3 already did.
   - **Fix:** None required. The conservative choice is deliberate, because widening it would promote real operator messages.

3. **Low — `cli/test/context_convergence.test.ts:1681` (the CTX-06 `doesNotMatch` can never fail).**
   - **Trigger:** `priorTasks` is rendered as one joined line, and the first entry in the fixture is "Review the cache layer". The regex is anchored on `Earlier task requests: \[Nexus ...`, so a leaked note in any later position still passes. That makes the negative assertion for continuation, recovery and empty-reply notes vacuous. The old assertion had the same weakness, and the rewrite did not strengthen it.
   - **Coverage elsewhere:** The new positive assertion (the prefixed operator text is listed) would fail under a prefix-based recogniser and does protect the intended property. `harness_provenance.test.ts` also covers the empty-reply note with `[\s\S]*`.
   - **Fix:** Use `assert.doesNotMatch(content, /Earlier task requests[^\n]*\[Nexus (?:continuation|recovery)|\[Nexus: your reply was empty/)`.

4. **Low (docs) — `docs/v1.1.4/FINDINGS.md:108`.** The row is marked FIXED but does not record the two residual limitations in items 1 and 2. Add one sentence naming them so the row stays truthful to its limits. Everything else in the row matches the code and tests.

## Tests
- `harness_provenance.test.ts` would fail with a prefix-based recogniser:
  - The `SPOOF` request is expected in "Earlier task requests", as the current request, and as the findings "Current request".
  - The marked note is expected to be excluded, and unanswered notes are expected to survive compaction.
  - The migration tests check idempotency, no mutation, never-first, and that `"[Nexus] hello"`, a note contained in other text, and a malformed counter are not migrated.
  - A table of the real v1.1.3 wordings is migrated.
- The test is not red-verified against base, since it imports a module that is absent there. It is nonetheless behavioural.

## Slop rating: **Low**
- The strongest positives are that there is a single recogniser with no duplicated logic, producers collapse into one helper, and the migration is anchored on the real wording and covered by a per-note-kind test.
- The only slop is the vacuous negative regex in CTX-06 and a long migration doc comment that restates the rule. There is no dead code, no stale references, and `SKILL_NOTE_PREFIX` was cleanly removed from `agent.ts`.

### Good
- Marker-only trust and conservative, position-aware migration with an explicit, documented tradeoff.
- Operator requests are now retained verbatim in compaction even with trailing notes.
- The sidecar history and the TUI share the same recogniser, which closes the earlier PR #467 Low row.

**2. Cross-piece interactions**
- Nexus session ids (`session_\d{8}_\d{6}_[a-z0-9]{1,8}`) are still exempt from the entropy rule. They don't contain `sk-`, and the Rust breadcrumb is printed directly without the redactor. So the resume hint cannot be corrupted.
- `isDottedKeyMaterial` needs every dot-separated segment to be at least 6 characters and key-like, and the whole token must also pass the entropy threshold. Paths with `/`, file names such as `.json`/`.jsonl`, versions, and `localhost:1234/v1` endpoints cannot match. Hosts like `api.us-east-1.amazonaws.com` fail because short segments such as `api` or `net` disqualify them.
- The new `saved` flag is a boolean and is not touched by redaction.
- #477's harness-note markers are metadata. The `emitHistory` change (marker-based hiding) and the `saved` change touch disjoint lines.

**3. Docs against the code at this head**
- README keeps "its work ledger is closed and qualification is pending" and says that development plans and the README do not establish release readiness.
- The docs do not mention #469, #434 or the TUI-only features of #472, such as the orientation card or exit breadcrumb.
- The approval claims ("review" is the default mode, `--yes` bypasses prompts, egress asks per origin) are about existing behaviour on main, not pieces missing from this branch.
- Redaction is described only as "secret redaction", with no broader guarantee.
- Every relative link in both README files resolves, and `make test-ci` exists on main via #418.

**4. Slop/Code/Security briefs on the combined diff**
- The only mechanical overlap between the pieces is `sidecar.ts`, and it is clean.
- Introduces nothing new in combination; the points below are residual and low-severity.
- The one thing I did not trace is a URL with several percent-encoded `/` and high entropy that is not path-like. It could be redacted unnecessarily, but that is fail-closed (over-redaction), and #478 already accepted it.
- The one thing to know about `saved` is that `session_saved |= saved` and the resume path set it true. A TS-side `saved:false` followed by a successful turn re-enables the breadcrumb. That is intended and tested.

**Suggestions (non-blocking)**
- `cli/src/core/egress.ts:~495-575` — the long comments on the extension budget and `scanBase64Keys` run to many lines of narration. They are accurate but verbose. Trim them to the invariant ("redaction only grows; extensions are budgeted").
- `cli/src/core/egress.ts:~813-820` — the `replaceValues` doc has a long line that wasn't rewrapped. Rewrap it.

**Slop: Low.** The two strongest reasons: the integration is mechanical and byte-accountable, and the redactor change comes with a monotonicity corpus test and negative and time-bound tests. The only residue is comment verbosity.

## Code Review
No defect at medium or higher.

Traced and held:
- **Esc ordering:** Esc is handled before the typing-run, shown and guard checks (`live_state.rs:1475`), so it denies even on an unshown panel or a too-small terminal.
- **Burst handling:** `on_key_burst_at` (`live_state.rs:1417`) re-arms the guard and marks `last_text`, so `y`, `n`, `a` and Enter in a batch fall into the typing branch. Esc still denies, and arrows and Backspace act normally. Order is preserved, and `find_map` stops at the first action so later keys in the batch are dropped.
- **Burst drain in the event loop:** the drain at `live.rs:496` keeps events in order and puts a non-key event into `pending`.
- **Read-all rule:** `seen_through` advances only when a drawn frame's `approval_top` is at or below it (`live_state.rs:1380`), so End never marks skipped rows. A width change resets it (`live_state.rs:1376`). A frame that is not drawn cannot set `read_all`.
- **Approve-all unseen:** `a` and the confirming Enter both hit the `!read_all` refusal (`live_state.rs:1550`) before the confirm logic.
- **Queued second approval:** it is promoted unshown with `read_all` false, and `last_key` from the answering key arms the opening guard.
- **Bracketed paste:** a paste arms `last_text` and the guard, and a queued burst at opening is discarded.
- **Shared predicate:** `visible_control` is unchanged from the base. The diff only adds `approval_visible_control` and `approval_safe_text`, and a test pins that prose such as `⚠️ done ok` is unchanged.
- **One render choke point:** `review_lines` runs `safe_row` over every row before the patch is parsed, so diff bodies are covered too. The heading, tool verb, subject and file path also go through `approval_safe_text` in `live_view.rs`.
- **Tiny terminals:** the arithmetic is saturating, so I found no underflow. A terminal too small shows only the refusal, and Esc denies.

## Findings (all Low)
1. **Weakened test, confirmed.** `live_view.rs:2761-2775`: `a_cramped_approval_keeps_the_action_heading_before_the_review` asserts `contains("Edit src/x.txt") || contains("enlarge the terminal")` plus `!contains("+B")`. At the base it was `assert_shows(…, &amp;["Edit src/x.txt"])`. The name now promises more than the check, and the `||` passes on the refusal alone.
   - Trigger: any height where the panel refuses and no heading is drawn.
   - Fix: assert the heading at the heights where the panel is drawn, and the refusal text at the heights where it is not. Or rename the test to match what it asserts.
2. **Untruthful count.** `docs/v1.1.4/TUI-AUDIT-STATUS.md`, D-01 row: "over 13 review kinds". `approval_sweep.rs:73-89` has 15 kinds. Fix the number.
3. **Debris.** `approval_sweep.rs:463-467` has five trailing blank lines. Remove them.

## Gaps the sweep does not cover (no defect found)
- **Event-loop grouping:** the sweep drives `on_key_burst_at` directly, so the `event::poll` grouping in `live.rs:496` is untested here. Only the `drain_key_presses` unit test and the PTY driver cover it.
- **Interleaved events:** a resize or mouse event between machine-speed keys splits the burst, and the keys behind it are then handled singly. This needs unusual timing, so I rate it negligible.
- **Resize mid-approval:** only unit tests cover it (`live_view.rs:4441`, `live_state.rs:4867`). It fails safe: a new width resets `seen_through`.
- **Width and wide characters:** the sweep uses widths of 20 and above only and has no CJK rows. Width below 20 is not swept, and a wide character at width 1 can draw one column over, but ratatui clips it. The row count at a fixed width is deterministic, so `seen_through` stays consistent.
- **Enter on Deny:** Enter on a focused Deny is subject to the guard and typing-run checks. Only Esc is immediate. The manual only says Esc denies "at any time", so this is accurate.
- **Look-alike characters:** the predicate covers invisible and bidi characters, not Cyrillic-style look-alikes. The manual does not claim that.
- **Stand-in ambiguity:** a file that literally contains the text `&lt;U+200B&gt;` looks the same as a real zero-width space.

## Manual, FINDINGS K, status rows, #476
- **Manual:** each approval sentence matches the code or a test that asserts it, including the 60x11 one-line command (sweep) and 40x11 one-line file change (`live_view.rs:4165-4170`). The one loose claim is "Keys with Ctrl, Alt, Super or Meta never answer", because Alt+Esc or Ctrl+Esc still denies. That is the safe direction, so I would not change the manual for it.
- **FINDINGS.md K:** the tests it names exist (`live_view.rs:4441`, `4481`; `live_state.rs:4613`, `4628`). The clippy row about `origin/main` is unrelated lint debt in a PR-review section, and I did not verify it.
- **#476:** it is the base commit and nothing in this diff touches it. The only CLI changes are test drivers.
